S7.2
Uniform Error bounds for
V_{u,d,\ell_{\star}}/\mathbb{E}\left(V_{u,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)-1
on
\Ecal_{n}

We consider the two cases
u=0
and
u=1
separately. The
u=0
case is used for showing the convergence of
\widehat{\tau}_{n}
in (
14
) and the
u=1
case is used for showing the convergence of
\widehat{\theta}_{n}
in (
14
).

Case B1.
If
u=0
, we write
\widetilde{W}=\big(W_{1},\ldots,W_{|\Xi_{0,m}|}\big)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
where

\displaystyle\left\{W_{1},\ldots,W_{|\Xi_{0,m}|}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})\right)}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)}}:\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\in\Xi_{0,m}\right\}.

Define
\mu_{W}=\mathbb{E}\big(\widetilde{W}\big)
,
\Sigma_{W}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{W}-\mu_{W}\big)\big(\widetilde{W}-\mu_{W}\big)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Big]
. We can write

\displaystyle\frac{V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)}=\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\widetilde{Z}+2\mu^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{W}\Sigma_{W}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}+\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W},

(S.99)

where
\widetilde{Z}=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{|\Xi_{0,m}|})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\sim\Ncal\left({0}_{|\Xi_{0,m}|},I_{|\Xi_{0,m}|}\right)
.

Therefore, using the upper bounds of
\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W}
and
\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}
proved in the later Section
S7.3
, (
S7.1
), (
S.98
) and (
S7.1
) imply that on the set
\Ecal_{n}
, as
m\to\infty
(equivalently
n=m^{d}\to\infty
),

\displaystyle\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}(V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}})}\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\Xi_{0,m}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}\mathrm{m}\big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})\big)\mathrm{m}\big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\big)

\displaystyle=O(1)\frac{\|\beta\|_{1}^{2}}{\tau}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\ell_{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\frac{p\|\beta\|^{2}}{\theta}\frac{\theta}{\tau}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\ell_{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{\rho_{1}+\rho_{21}-2(1-\gamma)\ell_{\star}/d}=o(1),

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cm^{-d}+C\frac{\theta^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d}\left(1+[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{n^{-1}+n^{2\rho_{21}-(1-\gamma)(4\nu+d)/d}\log^{2}n\right\}=o(1),

(S.100)

where the two
o(1)
’s are based on the order of
\omega_{m}=\lfloor m^{\gamma}\rfloor
, the upper bounds of
\|\beta_{1}\|^{2}/\theta
,
\tau/\theta
and
\alpha
in the set
\Ecal_{n}
, and the condition (
3.1
).
Hence
\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\mu_{W}\leq\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W}\big\|\Sigma_{W}\big\|_{F}=o(1)
as well.

Notice that taking expectation on both sides of (
S.99
) implies that
1=\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\widetilde{Z}\right)+\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W}
, and hence
\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\widetilde{Z}\right)=1-\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W}
. We conclude that for any
\epsilon>0
, for all sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle\quad~\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}}-1\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\widetilde{Z}+\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W}-1\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\widetilde{Z}-\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\widetilde{Z}\right)\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}2\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{\mathrm{HW}}}{16}\min\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}^{2}},\frac{\epsilon}{\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}}\right)\right\}+2\exp\left\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{16\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}\mu_{W}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{\mathrm{HW}}}{16}\min\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}^{2}},\frac{\epsilon}{\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}}\right)\right\}+2\exp\left\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{16\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{W}\big\|\Sigma_{W}\big\|_{F}}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}2\exp\left\{-C\varphi\left(\min\left\{n^{1/2},n^{(1-\gamma)(4\nu+d)/(2d)-\rho_{21}}/\log n\right\}\epsilon\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+2\exp\left\{-Cn^{2(1-\gamma)\ell_{\star}/d-\rho_{1}-\rho_{21}}\min\left\{n^{1/2},n^{(1-\gamma)(4\nu+d)/(2d)-\rho_{21}}/\log n\right\}\epsilon^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big\{-C\varphi\Big(\min\Big\{n^{1/2},~~n^{(1-\gamma)(4\nu+d)/(2d)-\rho_{21}}/\log n,

\displaystyle\qquad n^{1/4+(1-\gamma)\ell_{\star}/d-(\rho_{1}+\rho_{21})/2},~~n^{(1-\gamma)(4\nu+d+4\ell_{\star})/(4d)-(\rho_{1}+2\rho_{21})/2}/\log^{1/2}n\Big\}\epsilon\Big)\Big\},

(S.101)

for some constant
C>0
, where in the inequality (i), the first term follows from the Hanson-Wright inequality in Lemma
S.8
and
\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{\op}\leq\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}
, and the second term follows from the sub-Gaussian concentration inequality for the Gaussian random variable
\mu_{W}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{W}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}
; the inequality (ii) follows from (
S7.2
) and (
S7.3
) in Section
S7.3
.

Case B2.
If
u=1
, we write
\widetilde{U}=\big(U_{1},\ldots,U_{2|\Xi_{1,m}|}\big)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
, where

\displaystyle\left\{U_{1},\ldots,U_{|\Xi_{1,m}|}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}))}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)}}:\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\in\Xi_{1,m}\right\},

\displaystyle\left\{U_{|\Xi_{1,m}|+1},\ldots,U_{2|\Xi_{1,m}|}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}))}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)}}:\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\in\Xi_{1,m}\right\}.

Define the
2|\Xi_{1,m}|\times 2|\Xi_{1,m}|
symmetric matrix
A=(a_{i,k})_{1\leq i,k\leq 2|\Xi_{1,m}|}
by

\displaystyle a_{j,|\Xi_{1,m}|+j}

\displaystyle=a_{|\Xi_{1,m}|+j,j}=1/2,\qquad\text{for }j=1,\ldots,|\Xi_{1,m}|,

\displaystyle a_{j,k}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\mbox{otherwise.}

Then

\displaystyle\frac{V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}})}=\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}A\widetilde{U}.

Define
\mu_{U}=\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{U}\right)
and
\Sigma_{U}=\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{U}-\mu_{U})(\widetilde{U}-\mu_{U})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right]
.
We observe that

\displaystyle\frac{V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)}=\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}+2\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}+\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U},

(S.102)

where
\widetilde{Z}=\big(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{2|\Xi_{1,m}|}\big)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\sim\Ncal\left(0_{|\Xi_{0,m}|},I_{2|\Xi_{1,m}|}\right)
.

Therefore, using the upper bounds of
\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}
,
\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}\right\|_{F}
, and
\left\|\Sigma_{U}\right\|_{F}
proved in the later Section
S7.3
, and from (
S7.1
), (
S.89
) and (
S.96
), we have that on the event
\Ecal_{n}
, as
m\to\infty
(equivalently
n=m^{d}\to\infty
),

\displaystyle\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)}\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathrm{m}\big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})\big)\mathrm{m}\big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}\mathrm{m}\big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})\big)\mathrm{m}\big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=O(1)\frac{\|\beta\|_{1}^{2}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\ell_{\star}-2\nu}

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\frac{p\|\beta\|^{2}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\ell_{\star}-2\nu}

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{\rho_{1}-(1-\gamma)(2\ell_{\star}-2\nu)/d}=o(1),

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg\{\frac{\tau^{2}}{\theta^{2}}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-4\nu}+\frac{\tau}{\theta}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-2\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(1+[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{d}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{n^{2\rho_{22}-1+4(1-\gamma)\nu/d}+n^{-(1-\gamma)}\log^{2}n\right\}=o(1),

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{U}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg\{\frac{\tau^{2}}{\theta^{2}}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-4\nu}+\frac{\tau}{\theta}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-2\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(1+[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{d}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{n^{2\rho_{22}-1+4(1-\gamma)\nu/d}+n^{-(1-\gamma)}\log^{2}n\right\}=o(1),

(S.103)

where the three
o(1)
’s are based on the order of
\omega_{m}=\lfloor m^{\gamma}\rfloor
, the upper bounds of
\|\beta_{1}\|^{2}/\theta
,
\tau/\theta
and
\alpha
in the set
\Ecal_{n}
, and the condition (
3.1
).
Hence
\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}A\Sigma_{U}A^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}\leq\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}\big\|\Sigma_{U}\big\|_{F}=o(1)
as well.
Notice that taking expectation on both sides of (
S.102
) implies that
1=\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}\right)+\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}
, and hence
\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}\right)=1-\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}
. Then similar to the derivation of (
S7.2
), we conclude from (
S7.2
), (
S7.3
) and (
S7.3
) in Section
S7.3
that for any
\epsilon>0
, for all sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle\quad~\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)}-1\right|>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}\widetilde{Z}-\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}-1\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mu^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{U}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}\widetilde{Z}-\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}\widetilde{Z}\right)\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mu^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}_{U}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{\mathrm{HW}}}{16}\min\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\big\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}\big\|_{F}^{2}},\frac{\epsilon}{\big\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}\big\|_{F}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+2\exp\left\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{16\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}A\Sigma_{U}A^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{C_{\mathrm{HW}}}{16}\min\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\big\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}\big\|_{F}^{2}},\frac{\epsilon}{\big\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}\big\|_{F}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+2\exp\left\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{16\mu_{U}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}AA^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mu_{U}\left\|\Sigma_{U}\right\|_{F}}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}2\exp\left\{-C\varphi\left(\min\left\{n^{1/2-\rho_{22}-2(1-\gamma)\nu/d},n^{(1-\gamma)/2}/\log n\right\}\epsilon\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+2\exp\left\{-Cn^{(1-\gamma)(2\ell_{\star}-2\nu)/d-\rho_{1}}\min\left\{n^{1/2-2(1-\gamma)\nu/d-\rho_{22}},n^{(1-\gamma)/2}/\log n\right\}\epsilon^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big\{-C\varphi\Big(\min\Big\{n^{1/2-\rho_{22}-2(1-\gamma)\nu/d},~~n^{(1-\gamma)/2}/\log n,

\displaystyle\qquad n^{1/4+(1-\gamma)(\ell_{\star}-2\nu)/d-(\rho_{1}+\rho_{22})/2},~~n^{(1-\gamma)/4+(1-\gamma)(\ell_{\star}-\nu)/d-\rho_{1}/2}/\log^{1/2}n\Big\}\epsilon\Big)\Big\},

(S.104)

for some constant
C>0
, where the inequality (i) follows from (
S7.3
) and (
S7.3
) in Section
S7.3
.

S7.3
Bounds for the Frobenius Norms of
\big\|\Sigma_{W}\big\|_{F}^{2}
,
\big\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{W}\big\|_{F}^{2}
and
\big\|\Sigma_{U}\big\|_{F}^{2}

This section provides the detailed derivation of upper bounds for
\big\|\Sigma_{W}\big\|_{F}^{2}
,
\big\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{W}\big\|_{F}^{2}
, and
\big\|\Sigma_{U}\big\|_{F}^{2}
.

(1) Upper bound for
\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}^{2}
.

We first use
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
to obtain that

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{W}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[\tau\Ical\left\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right\}+\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)\Big]\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Ical\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\}\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)\Bigg\}^{2}.

(S.105)

For the first term in (
S7.3
), from Lemma
S.9
, we observe that as
m\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\quad~\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}}\left\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}\Ical\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}\left[c_{d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}+O(\omega_{m}^{-1})\right]\left[c_{d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}+O(\omega_{m}^{-1})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Ical\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\}\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cm^{d},

(S.106)

for some
C>0
that only depends on
\ell_{\star},\nu,d
.

For the second term in (
S7.3
), we consider two cases,
\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|\leq 5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}
and
\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}
. For the first case, the number of such
(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})
pairs is at most of order
m^{d}\omega_{m}^{d}
, which implies that

\displaystyle\quad~\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|\leq 5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|\leq 5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\Big\{\zeta_{\ell_{\star}}(-1)^{\ell_{\star}}(2\ell_{\star})!\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\ell_{\star}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigg[\sum_{j=\ell_{\star}+1}^{\infty}\zeta_{j}\|\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\|^{2j}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{*}_{\nu+j}G_{\nu+j}(\|\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\|)\Bigg]\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{2}m^{d}\omega_{m}^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu},

(S.107)

as
m\to\infty
for some
C>0
dependent only on
\ell_{\star},\nu,d
.

For the second case of
\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}
, if we let
\mathsf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, then we have that

\displaystyle\quad~\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Bigg[\sum_{0\leq a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}\leq 2\ell_{\star}-1}\frac{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}))}{a_{1}!\ldots a_{d}!}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\prod_{q=1}^{d}\left[s_{q}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-s_{q}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})-s_{q}(\mathbf{i})+s_{q}(\mathbf{j})\right]^{a_{q}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}=2\ell_{\star}}\frac{2\ell_{\star}}{a_{1}!\ldots a_{d}!}\prod_{q=1}^{d}\left[s_{q}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-s_{q}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})-s_{q}(\mathbf{i})+s_{q}(\mathbf{j})\right]^{a_{q}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{2\ell_{\star}-1}\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\Big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})

\displaystyle\qquad+t\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})+\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})\}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}=2\ell_{\star}}\frac{2\ell_{\star}}{a_{1}!\ldots a_{d}!}\prod_{q=1}^{d}\left[s_{q}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-s_{q}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})-s_{q}(\mathbf{i})+s_{q}(\mathbf{j})\right]^{a_{q}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{2\ell_{\star}-1}\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\Big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})

\displaystyle\qquad+t\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})+\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})\}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}C\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\ell_{\star}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\Bigg\{\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{2d}\leq\ell_{\star}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq t\leq 1\\
a_{1}+\ldots a_{d}=2\ell_{\star}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{D}^{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\Big(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})

\displaystyle\qquad+t\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})+\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})\}\Big)\Bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}C\theta^{2}(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\ell_{\star}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\left\{\|\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})\|-\frac{(4\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}+2)\sqrt{d}}{m}\right\}^{4\nu-4\ell_{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{2}(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\ell_{\star}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\|\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j})\|^{4\nu-4\ell_{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{2}(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\ell_{\star}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{0,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell_{\star}\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\left(\frac{\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|}{m}\right)^{4\nu-4\ell_{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{2}(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\ell_{\star}}\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\Xi_{0,m}}m^{d}\int_{\omega_{m}/m}^{\sqrt{d}}s^{4\nu-4\ell_{\star}+d-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}C\theta^{2}(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}m^{2d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d},

(S.108)

as
m\to\infty
, where the inequality (i) follows because all
s_{q}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m}),s_{q}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m}),s_{q}(\mathbf{i}),s_{q}(\mathbf{j})\in[0,1]
and the power
2\ell_{\star}-1=2\lceil\nu+d/2\rceil-1\geq 2-1=1
,
the inequality (ii) follows from Lemma
S.10
, and (iii) follows from
|\Xi_{0,m}|\leq m^{d}
and the definition that
\ell_{\star}=\lceil\nu+d/2\rceil\geq\nu+d/2>\nu+d/4
so
4\nu-4\ell_{\star}+d<0
.

Therefore, we combine (
S7.1
), (
S.98
), (
S7.3
), (
S7.3
), (
S7.3
) and (
S7.3
) to conclude that for all sufficiently large
m
,

\displaystyle\big\|\Sigma_{W}\big\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\tau C_{V,0})^{2}}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{0,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\cdot\frac{2}{(\tau C_{V,0})^{2}}\Bigg\{C\tau^{2}m^{d}+C\theta^{2}m^{d}\omega_{m}^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+C\theta^{2}(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}m^{2d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{C}{\tau^{2}m^{2d}}\Bigg\{\tau^{2}m^{d}+\theta^{2}m^{d}\omega_{m}^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\theta^{2}(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}m^{2d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=C\left\{m^{-d}+\frac{\theta^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d}\left(1+(1+|\log(\alpha)|)^{2}\right)\right\},

(S.109)

where the inequality (i) follows from (
S7.1
), (
S7.1
), and (
S.98
).

(2) Upper bound for
\|\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}A\Sigma^{1/2}_{U}\|_{F}^{2}
and
\|\Sigma_{U}\|_{F}^{2}
.

Based on the definition of
\Sigma_{U}
and
A
, we partition them into
2\times 2
blocks of size
|\Xi_{1,m}|\times|\Xi_{1,m}|
as follows:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{U}=\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}&\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\\
\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}&\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad A=\begin{pmatrix}A^{1,1}&A^{1,2}\\
A^{2,1}&A^{2,2}\end{pmatrix},

where
\Sigma_{U}^{j,k}
and
A^{j,k}
are
|\Xi_{1,m}|\times|\Xi_{1,m}|
matrices for
j,k\in\{1,2\}
. From the definition of
A
, we have
A^{1,1}=A^{2,2}=0\times I_{|\Xi_{1,m}|}
and
A^{1,2}=A^{2,1}=I_{|\Xi_{1,m}|}
. Consequently, we observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tr\left(A\Sigma_{U}A\Sigma_{U}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\tr\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}&\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\\
\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}&\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}&\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\\
\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}&\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\end{pmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\tr\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}+\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}&\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}+\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\\
\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}+\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}&\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}+\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\end{pmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{\tr\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\right)+\tr\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\right)\right\}.

(S.110)

We further observe that

\displaystyle\quad~\tr\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\right)=\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq|\Xi_{1,m}|}\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,1}\right)_{ij}\left(\Sigma_{U}^{2,2}\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}))\right]\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}))\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}))\right]\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1}))\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{4d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{2d+1},\ldots,k_{3d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{3d+1},\ldots,k_{4d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\tau\Ical\left\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right\}+\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[\tau\Ical\left\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\right)\Big],

(S.111)

and similarly

\displaystyle\quad~\tr\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\right)=\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq|\Xi_{1,m}|}\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\right)_{ij}\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\right)_{ji}=\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq|\Xi_{1,m}|}\left(\Sigma_{U}^{1,2}\right)_{ij}\left(\Sigma_{U}^{2,1}\right)_{ji}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}))\right]\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1}))\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}))\right]\left[\nabla_{d,\ell_{\star}}Y(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}))\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}\right)\right\}^{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{4d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{2d+1},\ldots,k_{3d})}c_{\mathbf{j},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{3d+1},\ldots,k_{4d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\tau\Ical\left\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right\}+\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[\tau\Ical\left\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\right)\Big].

(S.112)

Let
\{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\}=\{0_{|\Xi_{1,m}|},\mathbf{e}_{1}\}
. Then by (
S7.3
), we have that

\displaystyle\tau^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{4d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{2d+1},\ldots,k_{3d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{3d+1},\ldots,k_{4d})}

\displaystyle\times\Ical\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\}\Ical\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\tau^{2}m^{d},

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{4d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{2d+1},\ldots,k_{3d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{3d+1},\ldots,k_{4d})}

\displaystyle\times\Ical\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\right)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\tau\theta m^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\nu},

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{4d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{2d+1},\ldots,k_{3d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2},d,\ell_{\star}}^{(k_{3d+1},\ldots,k_{4d})}

\displaystyle\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)\Ical\{\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})=\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\tau\theta m^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\nu},

(S.113)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can obtain from (
S7.3
), (
S7.3
) and (
S7.3
) that for all sufficiently large
m
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|\leq 5\ell\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{4d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1},d,\ell}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell}^{(k_{2d+1},\ldots,k_{3d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2},d,\ell}^{(k_{3d+1},\ldots,k_{4d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{2}m^{d}\omega_{m}^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu},

(S.114)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}\in\Xi_{1,m}:\|\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}\|>5\ell\omega_{m}\sqrt{d}}\sum_{0\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{4d}\leq\ell_{\star}}c_{\mathbf{i},d,\ell}^{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1},d,\ell}^{(k_{d+1},\ldots,k_{2d})}c_{\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1},d,\ell}^{(k_{2d+1},\ldots,k_{3d})}c_{\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2},d,\ell}^{(k_{3d+1},\ldots,k_{4d})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{k}_{1}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}\omega_{m})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}\left(\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{i}+\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{3}\omega_{m})-\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{j}+\mathbf{b}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{4}\omega_{m})\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{2}[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}m^{2d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d}.

(S.115)

We combine (
S.89
), (
S.96
), (
S7.3
), (
S7.3
), (
S7.3
), (
S7.3
), (
S.114
), (
S.115
) to conclude that for all sufficiently large
m
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}A\Sigma_{U}^{1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left[\theta g_{\ell_{\star},\nu}\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left(V_{1,d,\ell_{\star}}^{2}\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\left[\theta g_{\ell_{\star},\nu}\right]^{2}}\times\Bigg\{C\tau^{2}m^{d}+C\tau\theta m^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\theta^{2}m^{d}\omega_{m}^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu}+C\theta^{2}[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}m^{2d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(1+o(1))}{m^{2d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu}\left[\theta g_{\ell_{\star},\nu}\right]^{2}}\Bigg\{\tau^{2}m^{d}+\tau\theta m^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{2\nu}+\theta^{2}m^{d}\omega_{m}^{d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad+\theta^{2}[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}m^{2d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{4\nu+d}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg\{\frac{\tau^{2}}{\theta^{2}}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-4\nu}+\frac{\tau}{\theta}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-2\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(1+[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{d}\Bigg\}.

(S.116)

For
\|\Sigma_{U}\|_{F}^{2}=\tr\big[(\Sigma_{U}^{1,1})^{2}\big]+\tr\big[(\Sigma_{U}^{2,2})^{2}\big]+\tr\big[(\Sigma_{U}^{1,2})^{2}\big]+\tr\big[(\Sigma_{U}^{2,1})^{2}\big]
, by the similar argument to above, we have that for all sufficiently large
m
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{U}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg\{\frac{\tau^{2}}{\theta^{2}}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-4\nu}+\frac{\tau}{\theta}m^{-d}\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{-2\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(1+[1+|\log(\alpha)|]^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\omega_{m}}{m}\right)^{d}\Bigg\}.

(S.117)

S7.4
A Lemma on the Derivatives of Matérn Covariance Function

Lemma S.10
.

For any
\mathsf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, the partial derivative
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{x})
is a continuous function. For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a positive constant
C_{\mathsf{D},\ell}
that only depends on
\ell,\nu,d
, such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t})\right|\leq C_{\mathsf{D},\ell}\theta\left(1+|\log\alpha|\right)\|\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t}\|^{2\nu-2\ell},

for all
\mathsf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfying
a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}=2\ell
and all
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}\in[0,1]^{d},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{t}
.

Proof of
Lemma
S.10
.

We consider two different cases below. For short, we write
r=\|\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t}\|
and
\overline{\mathsf{K}}(r;\theta,\alpha,\nu)=\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t})
. As such,
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t})
is the derivative of the composite function
\overline{\mathsf{K}}\circ r
.

We first cite an important result about the higher-order derivatives of composite functions. Proposition 1 of
Hardy 2006
has generalized this formula to the functions with multivariate arguments: for the composite function
f(g(\mathbf{x}))
with
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
and any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, its
k
th partial derivative satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{k}f(g(\mathbf{x}))}{\partial\tilde{x}_{1}\ldots\partial\tilde{x}_{k}}=\sum_{\pi}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}^{|\pi|}f}{\mathrm{d}y^{|\pi|}}\right)\Bigg|_{y=g(\mathbf{x})}\prod_{B\in\pi}\frac{\partial^{|B|}g(\mathbf{x})}{\prod_{j\in B}\partial\tilde{x}_{j}},

(S.118)

where each
\tilde{x}_{j}
’s represents some component in
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
, the sum is over all partition
\pi
of
\{1,\ldots,k\}
, the product is over all blocks
B
in the partition
\pi
, and
|\pi|
denotes the number of blocks in
\pi
. Proposition 2 of
Hardy 2006
has shown that even if some
\tilde{x}_{j}
’s correspond to the same component in
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
, (
S.118
) still holds true.

In this case, we take
f(\cdot)
to be
\overline{\mathsf{K}}(\cdot;\theta,\alpha,\nu)
and
g(\cdot)
to be the radius function
r(\cdot)
. By induction, it is straightforward to verify that for any
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
\mathsf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfying
a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}=k
, the partial derivatives of
r(\cdot)
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}r\right|\leq C_{k}^{\prime}/r^{k-1},

(S.119)

for some absolute constant
C_{k}^{\prime}>0
that only depends on
k,d
. Therefore, we conclude from (
S.118
) and (
S.119
) that for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
\mathsf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfying
a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}=2\ell
and all
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}\in[0,1]^{d},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{t}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t})\right|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{2\ell}C_{1k}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}r^{k}}\overline{\mathsf{K}}(r;\theta,\alpha,\nu)\right|\cdot r^{-(2\ell-k)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{2\ell}C_{1k}r^{k-2\ell}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}r^{k}}\overline{\mathsf{K}}(r;\theta,\alpha,\nu)\right|,

(S.120)

where
C_{1k}
’s are some positive constants that only depend on
\ell
,
0\leq k\leq\ell
, and
d
.

Next we bound the derivative of
\overline{\mathsf{K}}(\cdot)
in (
S7.4
). Since the series expansion in (
S.69
) is convergent for all radius
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we can differentiate term-by-term. We proceed in two different cases.

Case 1.
If
\alpha\leq 2
, then
\alpha\|\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t}\|\leq 2\sqrt{d}
. First consider the case
\nu\notin\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Then for any
r>0
, any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\quad~\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}r^{k}}\overline{\mathsf{K}}(r;\theta,\alpha,\nu)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\theta\left|\sum_{j=\lceil k/2\rceil}^{\infty}\xi_{j}\frac{(2j)!}{(2j-k)!}\alpha^{2j-2\nu}r^{2j-k}\right|+\theta\left|\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\xi_{\nu+j}^{*}\alpha^{2j}r^{2\nu-k+2j}\prod_{l=1}^{k}(2\nu+2j+1-l)\right|

\displaystyle=\theta r^{2\nu-k}\left\{\left|\sum_{j=\lceil k/2\rceil}^{\infty}\xi_{j}\frac{(2j)!}{(2j-k)!}(\alpha r)^{2j-2\nu}\right|+\left|\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\xi_{\nu+j}^{*}(\alpha r)^{2j}\prod_{l=1}^{k}(2\nu+2j+1-l)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\theta r^{2\nu-k}\Bigg\{\sum_{j=\lceil k/2\rceil}^{\infty}|\xi_{j}|\frac{(2j)!}{(2j-k)!}\left(2\sqrt{d}\right)^{2j-2\nu}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\xi_{\nu+j}^{*}|\left(2\sqrt{d}\right)^{2j}\prod_{l=1}^{k}|2\nu+2j+1-l|\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2k}\theta r^{2\nu-k},

(S.121)

for some large constant
C_{2k}
that depends only on
k,\nu,d
, where the last inequality follows because the two sequences are both convergent given the definition of
\xi_{j}
in (
S7
) and
\xi_{\nu+j}^{*}
and (
S7
).

If
\nu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, then we can similarly obtain that for a large absolute constant
C_{2k}
, for any
r>0
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}r^{k}}\overline{\mathsf{K}}(r;\theta,\alpha,\nu)\right|\leq C_{2k}\theta(1+|\log(\alpha)|)r^{2\nu-k}.

(S.122)

We combine (
S7.4
), (
S7.4
), and (
S.122
) to conclude that when
\alpha\leq 2
, for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
\mathsf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfying
a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}=2\ell
and all
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}\in[0,1]^{d},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{t}
,

\displaystyle\quad~\left|\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{2\ell}C_{1k}r^{k-2\ell}\cdot C_{2k}\theta(1+|\log(\alpha)|)r^{2\nu-k}

\displaystyle=\theta(1+|\log(\alpha)|)\sum_{k=1}^{2\ell}C_{1k}C_{2k}r^{2\nu-2\ell}=C_{3\ell}\theta(1+|\log(\alpha)|)r^{2\nu-2\ell},

(S.123)

where
C_{3\ell}=\sum_{k=1}^{2\ell}C_{1k}C_{2k}
.

Case 2.
If
\alpha\geq 2
, then we consider two different subcases below.

(i) If
\alpha\|\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t}\|\leq 2\sqrt{d}
, then similarly to Case 1, (
S7.4
) holds for any
r>0
. Hence (
S7.4
) remains true for this subcase.

(ii) For
\alpha\|\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t}\|>2\sqrt{d}
,
Paris 1984
has shown that for the modified Bessel function of the second kind
\Kcal_{\nu}
, it satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\Kcal_{\nu}(x)}{\Kcal_{\nu}(y)}>\mathrm{e}^{y-x}(x/y)^{\nu},\quad\text{for }\nu>-\frac{1}{2},~0<x<y.

Since
\alpha r=\alpha\|\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t}\|>2\sqrt{d}\geq 2
, we set
x=2
and
y=\alpha r
to obtain that

\displaystyle\Kcal_{\nu}(\alpha r)<(\alpha r/2)^{\nu}\mathrm{e}^{2-\alpha r}\Kcal_{\nu}(2)=2^{-\nu}\mathrm{e}^{2}\Kcal_{\nu}(2)(\alpha r)^{\nu}\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha r}.

(S.124)

On the other hand,
Watson 1944
has proved that (on page 79)

\displaystyle[x^{\nu}\Kcal_{\nu}(x)]^{(1)}=-x^{\nu}\Kcal_{\nu-1}(x),\qquad\text{for any }~\nu\in\mathbb{R},~x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.

where the superscript
(k)
denotes the
k
th derivative for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Let
h_{\nu}(r)=r^{\nu}\Kcal_{\nu}(\alpha r)
for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. By induction, we can see that for any integer
s\in\mathbb{N}
and any
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle h^{(2s)}_{\nu}(r)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{s}c_{1,k,s}\alpha^{s+k}r^{\nu-s+k}\Kcal_{\nu-s-k}(\alpha r),

\displaystyle h^{(2s+1)}_{\nu}(r)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{s}c_{2,k,s}\alpha^{s+k+1}r^{\nu-s+k}\Kcal_{\nu-s-k-1}(\alpha r),

(S.125)

where
c_{1,k,s}
’s and
c_{2,k,s}
’s are finite constants dependent on
k,s,\nu
only.

From (
S7.4
), for
\alpha r>2
and any
s\in\mathbb{N}
, we can find a finite constant
C_{4s}=\sum_{k=0}^{s}|c_{1,k,s}|>0
that depends only on
s,\nu,d
, such that

\displaystyle\left|h_{\nu}^{(2s)}(r)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{s}|c_{1,k,s}|\alpha^{s+k}r^{\nu-s+k}\Kcal_{\nu-s-k}(\alpha r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\sum_{k=0}^{s}|c_{1,k,s}|\alpha^{s+k}r^{\nu-s+k}\Kcal_{|s+k-\nu|}(\alpha r)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}2^{-\nu}\mathrm{e}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{s}|c_{1,k,s}|\alpha^{s+k}r^{\nu-s+k}\cdot\Kcal_{|s+k-\nu|}(2)(\alpha r)^{|s+k-\nu|}\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha r}

\displaystyle=2^{-\nu}\mathrm{e}^{2}\alpha^{\nu}r^{2\nu-2s}\sum_{k=0}^{s}|c_{1,k,s}|\Kcal_{|s+k-\nu|}(2)(\alpha r)^{|s+k-\nu|+(s+k-\nu)}\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha r}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}C_{4s}2^{-\nu}\mathrm{e}^{2}\alpha^{\nu}r^{2\nu-2s}(s+1)\left\{\max_{t\in\{0,1,\ldots,|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|\}}\Kcal_{t}(2)\right\}(\alpha r)^{2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)}\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha r}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{\leq}}C_{4s}2^{-\nu}\mathrm{e}^{2}(s+1)\lceil 2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)\rceil!\alpha^{\nu}r^{2\nu-2s}\left\{\max_{t\in\{0,1,\ldots,\lceil|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|\rceil\}}\Kcal_{t}(2)\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(v)}}{{\leq}}C_{5s}\alpha^{\nu}r^{2\nu-2s},

(S.126)

where (i) follows from the relation
\Kcal_{-s}(r)=\Kcal_{s}(r)
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
; (ii) follows from the upper bound in (
S.124
); (iii) follows from
\alpha r>2
and
|s+k-\nu|+(s+k-\nu)\leq 2|s+k-\nu|\leq 2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)
; (iv) follows because
\exp(x)>x^{s}/s!
for all
x>0
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we can set
x=\lceil 2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)\rceil
, such that

\displaystyle\exp(\alpha r)>(\alpha r)^{\lceil 2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)\rceil}/\lceil 2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)\rceil!

and hence

\displaystyle(\alpha r)^{2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)}\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha r}\leq\lceil 2(|s-\nu|+|2s-\nu|)\rceil!;

and the constant
C_{5s}
in (v) only depends on
s,\nu,d
but not
\alpha
and
r
.

Similarly, using the second relation in (
S7.4
), we can show that for
\alpha r>2
and any
s\in\mathbb{N}
, we can find a finite constant
C_{6s}>0
that depends on
c_{2,k,s},s,\nu,d
, such that

\displaystyle\left|h_{\nu}^{(2s+1)}(r)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{6s}\alpha^{\nu}r^{2\nu-2s-1}.

(S.127)

Therefore, using the definition
\overline{\mathsf{K}}(r;\theta,\alpha,\nu)=2^{1-\nu}\Gamma(\nu)^{-1}\theta\alpha^{-\nu}r^{\nu}\Kcal_{\nu}(\alpha r)=2^{1-\nu}\Gamma(\nu)^{-1}\theta\alpha^{-\nu}h_{\nu}(r)
, we combine (
S7.4
), (
S7.4
), and (
S.127
) to conclude that when
\alpha\|\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t}\|>2\sqrt{d}
, for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
\mathsf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfying
a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d}=2\ell
and all
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}\in[0,1]^{d},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{t}
,

\displaystyle\quad~\left|\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{a}}\theta K_{\alpha,\nu}(\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{\ell}C_{7s}r^{2s-2\ell}\cdot\frac{2^{1-\nu}\theta\alpha^{-\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)}\left|h_{\nu}^{(2s)}(r)\right|+\sum_{s=0}^{\ell-1}C_{8s}r^{2s+1-2\ell}\cdot\frac{2^{1-\nu}\theta\alpha^{-\nu}}{\Gamma(\nu)}\left|h_{\nu}^{(2s+1)}(r)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{\ell}\frac{2^{1-\nu}C_{7s}}{\Gamma(\nu)}\theta\alpha^{-\nu}r^{2s-2\ell}\cdot\alpha^{\nu}r^{2\nu-2s}+\sum_{s=0}^{\ell-1}\frac{2^{1-\nu}C_{8s}}{\Gamma(\nu)}\theta\alpha^{-\nu}r^{2s+1-2\ell}\cdot\alpha^{\nu}r^{2\nu-2s-1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{9\ell}\theta r^{2\nu-2\ell},

(S.128)

where
C_{7s},C_{8s},C_{9\ell}
are constants that depends only on
0\leq s\leq\ell
and
\ell,\nu,d
.

The conclusion is proved by combining Case 1 and Case 2.
∎

S8
Additional Numerical Results

Before we present the simulation results for
d=1
, we first explain the formulas for calculating the mean squared error for Bayesian posterior prediction and the oracle prediction. In the matrix format, our model can be written as
Y_{n}=F_{n}\beta+X_{n}+e_{n}
, where
X_{n}=(X(\mathbf{s}_{1}),\ldots,X(\mathbf{s}_{n}))^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
and
e_{n}=(\varepsilon(\mathbf{s}_{1}),\ldots,\varepsilon(\mathbf{s}_{n}))^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
. Since in the simulations, we assign the normal prior
\beta\sim\Ncal(0,a_{0}I_{p})
as in Proposition
2
, we can obtain that the conditional posterior distribution of
\beta
is

\displaystyle\beta\mid\theta,\alpha,\tau,Y_{n},F_{n}\sim\Ncal\left(\tilde{\beta},~\big[F_{n}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}F_{n}+a_{0}I_{p}\big]^{-1}\right),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}=\big[F_{n}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}F_{n}+a_{0}I_{p}\big]^{-1}F_{n}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}Y_{n}.

(S.129)

Under the model
Y\sim\gp(\beta^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathrm{f},\theta K_{\alpha}+\tau\delta_{0})
, by Section 1.5 of
Stein 1999
, the best linear unbiased predictor for
Y(\mathbf{s}^{*})
at a new location
\mathbf{s}^{*}\in[0,1]^{d}
based on the data
(Y_{n},F_{n})
is

\displaystyle\widehat{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau,\beta)

\displaystyle=\beta^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathrm{f}(\mathbf{s}^{*})+\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}\left(Y_{n}-F_{n}\beta\right),

(S.130)

where
K(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})=\{K(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}^{*}),\ldots,K(\mathbf{s}_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})\}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
is an
n\times 1
vector.

Under the Bayesian setup, we randomly draw
(\theta,\alpha,\tau,\beta)
from the posterior
\Pi(\cdot\mid Y_{n},F_{n})
to predict
Y(\mathbf{s}^{*})
. We denote the predicted variable as
\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})
. The Gaussian process predictive distribution implies that

\displaystyle\quad~\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\mid Y_{n},F_{n},\theta,\alpha,\tau,\beta

\displaystyle\sim\Ncal\left(\widehat{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau,\beta),~\theta\left\{K_{\alpha}(\mathbf{s}^{*},\mathbf{s}^{*})-\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}K_{\alpha}(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}\right).

(S.131)

From (
S8
), (
S.130
) and (
S8
), with some algebra, we can integrate out
\beta
from the posterior distribution and obtain that for any
\mathbf{s}^{*}\in[0,1]^{d}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\mid Y_{n},F_{n},\theta,\alpha,\tau\sim\Ncal\Big(Y^{\dagger}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau),\mathrm{v}_{n}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)\Big),\quad\text{where }

\displaystyle Y^{\dagger}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)=\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathrm{b}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left[F_{n}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}F_{n}+a_{0}I_{p}\right]^{-1}F_{n}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}Y_{n},

\displaystyle{\mathrm{v}}_{n}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)=\theta\left\{K_{\alpha}(\mathbf{s}^{*},\mathbf{s}^{*})-\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}K_{\alpha}(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathrm{b}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left[F_{n}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}F_{n}+a_{0}I_{p}\right]^{-1}\mathrm{b}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau),\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathrm{b}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)=\mathrm{f}(\mathbf{s}^{*})-F_{n}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\{\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n})+\tau I_{n}\big\}^{-1}\theta K_{\alpha}(S_{n},\mathbf{s}^{*}).

(S.132)

Let
Y_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})=\beta_{0}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathrm{f}(\mathbf{s}^{*})+X(\mathbf{s}^{*})
be the true mean function at
\mathbf{s}^{*}
without measurement error. Then, for the Bayesian posterior prediction
\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})
given a random draw
(\theta,\alpha,\tau)
from the posterior density
\pi(\theta,\alpha,\tau\mid Y_{n},F_{n})
, the mean squared error has the formula

\displaystyle M_{\text{post}}(\mathbf{s}^{*})

\displaystyle=\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\mid\theta,\alpha,\tau}\left\{\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}-Y_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right]^{2}+{\Var}_{\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\mid\theta,\alpha,\tau}\left\{\tilde{Y}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{Y^{\dagger}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)-\beta_{0}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathrm{f}(\mathbf{s}^{*})-X(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}^{2}+{\mathrm{v}}_{n}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau),

(S.133)

where
Y^{\dagger}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)
and
{\mathrm{v}}_{n}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta,\alpha,\tau)
are defined in (
S8
).

For the oracle prediction mean squared error of the predicted value
\tilde{Y}_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})
, we simply replace all
(\theta,\alpha,\tau)
in (
S8
) by the true parameters
(\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0})
to obtain that

\displaystyle M_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})

\displaystyle=\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{Y}_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\mid\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0}}\left\{\tilde{Y}_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}-Y_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right]^{2}+{\Var}_{\tilde{Y}_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\mid\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0}}\left\{\tilde{Y}_{0}(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{Y^{\dagger}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0})-\beta_{0}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathrm{f}(\mathbf{s}^{*})-X(\mathbf{s}^{*})\right\}^{2}+{\mathrm{v}}_{n}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0}),

(S.134)

where
Y^{\dagger}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0})
and
{\mathrm{v}}_{n}(\mathbf{s}^{*};\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0})
are defined in (
S8
) with
(\theta,\alpha,\tau)
replaced by
(\theta_{0},\alpha_{0},\tau_{0})
.

Now we present additional simulation results for the spatial Gaussian process model (
1
) with domain dimension
d=1
and the isotropic Matérn covariance function in (
2
). We still consider two values of the smoothness parameter
\nu=1/2
and
\nu=1/4
. The true covariance parameters are set to be
\theta_{0}=5,\alpha_{0}=1,\tau_{0}=0.5
for both
\nu=1/2
and
\nu=1/4
. For
d=1
and the domain
[0,1]
, we choose the sampling points
S_{n}
to be the regular grid
\mathbf{s}_{i}=(2i-1)/(2n)
for
i=1,\ldots,n
, where we choose
n=400,500,625,781,976,1220,1525,1907,2384,2980
, such that the sample size roughly follows the geometric sequence
n\approx 400\times 1.25^{k-1}
for
k=1,\ldots,10
. For the regression functions, we let
\mathrm{f}(\mathbf{s})=\left(1,\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{2},\mathbf{s}^{3}\right)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
for
\mathbf{s}\in[0,1]
and
\beta_{0}=(1,0.66,-1.5,1)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
. Other aspects of the Bayesian simulation setup, including the prior specification and the posterior sampling procedures are all the same as the
d=2
case described in the main text.

The results are summarized in Figure
S5
for
\nu=1/2
and Figure
S6
for
\nu=1/4
. In the left panels, the boxplots of both the marginal posterior distributions of
\theta
and
\tau
contract to the true parameter values
\theta_{0}=5
and
\tau_{0}=0.5
as
n
increases. The right panels show that the means of absolute differences from all posterior draws of
(\theta,\tau)
to the true parameters
(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
decreases approximately linearly in the sample size on the logarithm scale, indicating polynomial posterior contraction rates for
\theta
and
\alpha
.

Figure S5
:
Posterior contraction for
d=1
and
\nu=1/2
. Left column: Boxplots for the marginal posterior densities of
\theta
and
\tau
versus the increasing sample size
n
. The grey dashed lines are the true parameters
\theta_{0}=5
and
\tau_{0}=0.5
. Right column: Posterior means of
|\theta-\theta_{0}|
and
|\tau-\tau_{0}|
versus the increasing sample size
n
, on the logarithm scale. The dashed lines are the linear regression fits, and the grey shaded areas are the 95% confidence bands. All posterior summaries are averaged over 40 macro Monte Carlo replications.

Figure S6
:
Posterior contraction for
d=1
and
\nu=1/4
. Left column: Boxplots for the marginal posterior densities of
\theta
and
\tau
versus the increasing sample size
n
. The grey dashed lines are the true parameters
\theta_{0}=5
and
\tau_{0}=0.5
. Right column: Posterior means of
|\theta-\theta_{0}|
and
|\tau-\tau_{0}|
versus the increasing sample size
n
, on the logarithm scale. The dashed lines are the linear regression fits, and the grey shaded areas are the 95% confidence bands. All posterior summaries are averaged over 40 macro Monte Carlo replications.

Figure S7
:
Prediction mean squared errors for
\nu=1/2
and
\nu=1/4
when
d=1
. Left column: The prediction mean squared errors under both the Bayesian posterior prediction and the oracle prediction based on the true parameters. Right column: Ratios of the Bayesian prediction mean squared error and the oracle prediction mean squared error. All posterior summaries are averaged over 2000 testing locations in
[0,1]
and 40 macro Monte Carlo replications.

We also investigate the Bayesian posterior prediction performance for the
d=1
case and compare with the best possible prediction. We draw another
N=2000
points
\mathbf{s}_{1}^{*},\ldots,\mathbf{s}_{N}^{*}
uniformly from the domain
[0,1]
as the testing locations, and compute the prediction mean squared error
\mathbb{E}_{Y_{n}}\big\{N^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}M_{\text{post}}(\mathbf{s}_{l}^{*})\big\}
versus the oracle prediction mean squared error
\mathbb{E}_{Y_{n}}\big\{N^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}M_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{l}^{*})\big\}
. The results are shown in Figure
S7
. The left panel shows that the Bayesian predictive posterior has almost the same mean square errors as the oracle prediction for both
\nu=1/2
and
\nu=1/4
. The right panel shows that their ratio averaged over
N=2000
testing locations decreases towards 1 as the sample size
n
increases, indicating the asymptotic efficiency of Bayesian posterior prediction.
